Commits made: `8c0cdd1`, `9ff29ad`, `b3027ee`. Every commit passes on its own: 113, 123 and 123 unit and architecture tests. Rebasing changed nothing, because the branch is already on top of origin/main. The reviewer is running now. I'll push and open the PR once it's done and any Blocker or Major findings are fixed.

While that runs, here is the **W0.3b decision on where `Trade` lives**.

**The conflict:** Ledger owns trades. But Portfolio's and PnL's `domain/` code may import only the standard library and `di1.shared`, so it can't import `ledger/public.py`.

**Options:**
- **A. A shared `Trade` type in `di1/shared/trades.py`, with Ledger as owner (recommended).** This is lane E's version, which you already approved in that session.
  - Ledger's `public.py` re-exports `Trade` and adds `TradeRecord(trade, id, input_kind, created_at, deletion audit)`.
  - Its quantity is `signed_quantity: int`, which matches `schema.sql` (`INTEGER`).
- **B. `Trade` stays in `ledger/public.py`, and each consuming domain declares a structural `Protocol`.** This brings back the per-module copies you rejected.
- **C. Let domain code import other modules' `public.py`.** This breaks the domain-purity rule and the boundary tests, and risks import cycles.

**What I'd refine within A:**
1. **Narrow the "shared values" rule to lane B's wording.** A type goes in `di1.shared` only when the *domain* code of two or more modules computes on it. A type used only from application layers stays in its owner's `public.py`. So Pricing's `Quote` and `Price` stay in `pricing/public.py`.
2. **Move `InputKind` (PU or RATE) to `di1.shared`.** The Pricing, Ledger and Prices domains all validate on it.
3. **`CalculationCompleteness` stays in `pnl/public.py`.** Only the PnL domain computes it.
   - **Spec gap:** lane F's enum has `NOT_REQUIRED`, but the schema's `pnl_status` CHECK allows only `COMPLETE` and `MISSING`. That needs your ruling.
4. **Lane F switches to the shared `Trade`.** It drops its own `PnLTrade`, which uses a `Decimal` quantity.
5. **Pricing signature, which also needs your ruling.** Lane B's note says `calculate(instrument_id, reference_date, quote)`: the service reads the instrument itself, so no caller can pass a stale face value. But lane B's own edit to `interfaces.md` still says `instrument: InstrumentDescriptor`. I recommend `instrument_id`.
6. **Separate, in PR #24 for your review:** lane B's spec edit changes "100.000 constant" to "the numerator is `face_value`". That's the same for DI1, but it is a wording change to spec §4.1.

The full notes are in `lane-x-trade-placement-recommendation.md` in the shared notes folder.

**To decide:** A plus refinements 1–4? `instrument_id` for item 5? And how should `NOT_REQUIRED` fit with the schema's CHECK?

## Minor

**m1. The `DomainError` docstring contradicts the handler.** `backend/src/di1/shared/errors.py:45-46`
- **Rule:** backend/CLAUDE.md:68 says "One `DomainError` holds details of one status only (all 422, say); mixing statuses answers 500." `composition/errors.py:113-117` does exactly that, and `test_details_of_different_statuses_are_a_500_not_an_order_dependent_status` checks it.
- **Problem:** the docstring still says "The first detail's code decides the HTTP status, so raise details of one kind together." That describes the behaviour from before the earlier review fix.
- **Scenario:** a lane engineer trusts the docstring, raises `DomainError(ErrorDetail(VALIDATION_ERROR, …), ErrorDetail(NOT_FOUND, …))`, expects a 422 and gets a 500.
- **Same issue nearby:** the `code` property ("The code of the first detail", line 59-62) keeps that idea alive, and nothing in the diff uses it.
- **Fix:** reword the docstring to "All details must map to one HTTP status; mixing statuses is a programming error (500)." Drop `code`, or document it as informational only.

**m2. The mixed-status programming error is logged without a traceback.** `backend/src/di1/composition/errors.py:115`
- **Problem:** this path returns its own 500, so Starlette's `ServerErrorMiddleware` never re-raises or logs it. The only trace is a `logger.error` line with the details and no stack, so you cannot tell which service raised the bad combination.
- **Scenario:** a service in any lane mixes 404 and 422 details. Production logs show "DomainError mixes codes of different statuses: (...)" and nothing about where it came from.
- **Fix:** use `logger.error(..., exc_info=exc)`. The handler runs inside Starlette's `except` block, so `logger.exception` would also work.

**m3. `DomainError` cannot be copied or pickled.** `backend/s

Correction to my earlier flag: **there is no schema gap.** Lane F's `CalculationCompleteness` has only `COMPLETE` and `MISSING`, which matches every `pnl_status` CHECK. `NOT_REQUIRED` appears in the schema only in `position_metrics.valuation_status`, and its staged copy: a position valued for a day it doesn't need a price. Lane E's note mixed up the two statuses. The real question is how to type these statuses.

**Schema status columns:**

| Column | Allowed values |
|---|---|
| `position_metrics.pnl_status`, `portfolio_metrics.pnl_status` | COMPLETE, MISSING |
| `portfolio_metrics.valuation_status` | COMPLETE, MISSING |
| `position_metrics.valuation_status` | COMPLETE, MISSING, NOT_REQUIRED |

**Options:**
1. **Two enums, each matching its CHECK (recommended).**
   - `CalculationCompleteness {COMPLETE, MISSING}` goes in `pnl/public.py`. The PnL domain produces it, and Portfolio uses it for the portfolio valuation too.
   - `PositionValuationStatus {COMPLETE, MISSING, NOT_REQUIRED}` goes in `portfolio/public.py`, because Portfolio computes `financial_total`.
   - The types can't hold a value the database would reject, and the schema doesn't change.
2. **One three-value enum in `di1.shared`.** It's simpler, but a `NOT_REQUIRED` P&L status would type-check and then fail only at the database CHECK.
3. **Add `NOT_REQUIRED` to `pnl_status`.** This is a schema change with no use case: under OQ-10, an asset with no P&L to compute gets no row at all.

**📨 Message from session “Lead Backend”**

> New user decision to record as OQ-21: cash and positions become two separately processable chains. It also changes W0.3b and needs migration 0002.
>
> User's words, after reviewing the design: "I dont want cash calculated on the go. It should be precalculated. How could cash and position/trades be split in a way I can impact the cash if I want with a processing or only impact trades or both together." Then, after the design below: "looks good. Communicate the lanes".
>
> DECISION (record with `record-decision`; it amends spec §7.3, §8.2, §10, OQ-11, OQ-12, OQ-19 item 3, plus schema.md/schema.sql and interfaces.md):
>
> 1. Two independent daily chains read the same Ledger facts and never each other's output.
>    - CASH chain: `cash[D] = cash[D−1] − Σ(signed_qty × execution_PU) + deposits − withdrawals`. It reads trades and cash movements directly from Ledger, with no positions and no prices.
>    - POSITIONS chain: quantities, average PU/rate, then valuation (`financial_total`, market value) and P&L with prices.
>    - Their only meeting point is AUM = cash + market value.
> 2. Dirty markers per chain. Split `accounting_dirty_from` into `cash_dirty_from` and `positions_dirty_from`; `valuation_dirty_from` stays.
>    - trade create/delete on D → cash AND positions stale from D (a trade moves both);
>    - deposit/withdrawal → cash only;
>    - reference price → valuation only (unchanged).
> 3. Revisions per input. Split `ledger_revision` into `trades_revision` and `cash_movements_revision`.
>    - A CASH job fences on trades and cash movements.
>    - A POSITIONS job fences on trades and prices.
>    - So a deposit doesn't supersede a running positions job; a new trade supersedes both kinds.
> 4. `processing.requests.scope` ∈ {CASH, POSITIONS, ALL}, NOT NULL.
>    - Each scope checks its own A−1 predecessor and raises its own `STALE_PREDECESSOR`.
>    - Each fences its own revisions and advances only its own markers to B+1 (OQ-12).
>    - ALL does both, in one atomic publish.
>    - Still exactly [A, B], one active job, all-or-nothing publish (§10.4).
> 5. Storage:
>    - New published table `portfolio.cash_balances(balance_date PK, cash_balance, trades_cash_flow, deposits, withdrawals, updated_at)` plus its staging twin (Portfolio owns both, OQ-8).
>    - Drop `portfolio_metrics.cash_balance`.
>    - The AUM CHECK can no longer live in one row. Replace it with a publish invariant: whichever scope publishes recomputes `aum` for its days, reading the other chain's published value in the same transaction (safe because one job runs at a time).
>    - AUM is `null` when either side is missing, and reads stale when either side is stale. Add an integration test for `aum = cash + market value`.
> 6. Invalidator protocol in `processing/public.py`: replace `ledger_changed` with `trades_changed(effective_date, *, uow)` and `cash_movements_changed(effective_date, *, uow)`; keep `prices_changed`. `ProcessingRequest` gains `scope`.
> 7. UI (send lane I a request; don't edit frontend):
>    - the processing form and Processamentos get a scope choice (Caixa / Posições / Tudo), with pt-BR labels;
>    - the portfolio summary exposes cash freshness separately from positions/valuation;
>    - the Lançamentos strip reads the published cash from `cash_balances` with its own stale flag.
>
> Your tasks:
> - (a) Record OQ-21 and update every affected doc in one change. Show the user the diff first.
> - (b) Write migration 0002 (`db-migration` skill; apply on a fresh DB, upgrade from 0001, downgrade where safe).
> - (c) Adjust your W0.3b processing contract per item 6 before committing.
> - (d) Tell lane I about the contract changes.
>
> Lanes E and F are getting the domain file-split brief now. Lane E also builds `cash.py` and `valuation.py`. Don't start Processing or Portfolio persistence yourself.

  - §11.1 and §11.3: the scope in Processamentos, and the cash strip reading `cash_balances`.
  - §14: the closed-decisions line.
- **interfaces.md:**
  - Portfolio has two pure chains, and `publish_range` takes a `scope`.
  - The invalidator is `trades_changed`, `cash_movements_changed` and `prices_changed`.
  - `preview_range`, `enqueue_range` and `cancel_and_replace` take a `scope`.
  - The dirty-marker rule is per scope, and the UI read needs add a scope entry.
- **The plan, CLAUDE.md §2 and integrator.md** are updated to match.

**2. `feat(db): split cash and positions in the schema (migration 0002)`**, not staged yet, about 400 lines.
- `schema.sql`, `schema.md`, the `backend/CLAUDE.md` layout line.
- The migration: `0002_cash_chain.py`, plus its upgrade SQL and its downgrade SQL.
- Tests: `test_migration_sql.py` is reworked, and `test_cash_chain_migration.py` is new.

**3. W0.3b `processing/public.py` (task c):** the invalidator now has `trades_changed`, `cash_movements_changed` and `prices_changed`, and there's a new `ProcessingScope` enum. `ProcessingRequest.scope` is added, and the fake and tests are updated. Unit and architecture tests: 137 passed.

**Choices I made that you should check:**
- **A POSITIONS publish also advances `valuation_dirty_from`**, because valuation belongs to the positions chain. A CASH publish touches only `cash_dirty_from`.
- **Migrating existing data:**
  - existing requests become `ALL`;
  - both new revisions start from the old `ledger_revision`;
  - both new markers start from the old `accounting_dirty_from`.
  Published cash has no components to backfill, so 0002 drops it, sets `aum = NULL`, and marks cash stale from the first published day. It also clears staged rows, so it must run with no active job.
- **Downgrade to 0001** restores 0001's catalog exactly. Without `-x allow_data_loss=true`, it refuses while it would lose published cash, published portfolio metrics, staged cash, or a CASH/POSITIONS request.
- **The schema test changed form.** It used to check that `0001_initial.sql` equals `schema.sql`, which stops being true now. Instead it pins 0001's SHA-256 (a released revision is never edited) and checks that no revision SQL carries `BEGIN`/`COMMIT`. A new database test compares 0001 + 0002 against `schema.sql`, column by column, including constraints and indexes. I checked it fails when `schema.sql` is changed.
- **The row CHECK on `aum` is now `aum IS NULL OR positions_market_value IS NOT NULL`.** The equality `aum = cash + market value` can't span two tables, so it becomes a publish invariant.

**Verified:**
- `schema.sql` applies to a fresh PostgreSQL 16 database: 21 tables. The new CHECKs were probed: a missing or unknown scope, an `aum` without a market value, and a negative withdrawal were all rejected.

- `make test-backend`: "alembic heads: 0002 (head)", "241 passed".

**3. `docs(docs): record OQ-22, chain predecessors, issue chain, empty days`** (staged; 5 files, +29/−12)
- **OQ-22 row in open-questions.md**, with your exact words.
  1. If A−1 has no published cash but there are entries dated before A, a CASH or ALL job is refused when it starts. It ends `FAILED` with `CASH_PREDECESSOR_MISSING` and the pt-BR message "Caixa do dia anterior não processado: processe primeiro um intervalo anterior." Queuing is always allowed.
  2. If cash at A−1 is stale, the job runs and publishes flagged stale.
  3. Positions keep OQ-11.
  4. Each `STALE_PREDECESSOR` names its chain.
  5. A day with no expected asset is still processed: P&L 0, AUM = cash, cumulative P&L carried forward.
  6. There is no midnight process. You process D0 yourself, and D opens from D−1's close.
  7. Your confirmation of choices (a)–(c).
- **Spec:**
  - §7.1: the carry-forward.
  - §8.3: empty days.
  - §10.1: predecessor rules per chain.
  - §10.3: `FAILED` also covers `CASH_PREDECESSOR_MISSING`, and a stale-predecessor Pendência names its chain.
  - §13: new acceptance cases 13–15.
  - §14: the closed list.
  - The reviewer's wording nits.

**4. `feat(db): add issues.chain and guard migration 0002`** (8 files, +129/−20)
- **New column `processing.issues.chain`**, CASH or POSITIONS.
  - A CHECK requires it to be set exactly on `STALE_PREDECESSOR` issues.
  - It is part of the unique index, so an ALL job can record one issue per chain.
- **Review fixes to migration 0002:**
  - m1: neither upgrade nor downgrade runs while a job is active. Downgrade also guards the staged tables it empties.
  - m2: on downgrade, the two revisions are merged by sum instead of `GREATEST`, so a cash-only change can't be hidden.
  - m3: the test docstring and schema.md say why column order isn't compared.
  - m5: the db-migration skill gains a "later revisions" section, and the ci.yml comment is fixed.
- **New tests:**
  - an ALL job records both chains;
  - a stale-predecessor issue without a chain is rejected;
  - a duplicate is rejected;
  - an active job blocks the migration in both directions;
  - an existing stale-predecessor issue becomes POSITIONS.

**New commit 5: `docs(docs): describe portfolio's two chains and valuation as built`** (interfaces.md "Portfolio", working tree). It mirrors #28's code:
- Portfolio's own `Trade(asset_id, trade_date, trade_time, seq, signed_quantity, execution_pu, execution_rate)`, which the application layer maps from `ledger.public.TradeRecord`.
- **Positions chain:** `PositionsEngine.calculate_day/range`, using `PositionState`, `OpenPosition`, `PositionsDay` and `PositionDay`.
  - Rows exist exactly for the expected assets (OQ-10), and averages are `None` when flat.
  - Averages are rounded at the day's close (PU 10 dp, rate 12 dp) and carried forward as stored.
- **Cash chain:** `CashCalculator.calculate_day/range(previous_balance, trades, deposits, withdrawals, …)`.
  - It takes daily totals (`Mapping[date, Decimal]` for the range).
  - Each day produces a `CashDay(…, trades_cash_flow, …, cash_balance)`, which becomes one `cash_balances` row.
- **Valuation:** `ValuationCalculator.calculate_day(date, assets, cash_balance)`.
  - A PU and its `reference_price_id` go together (OQ-9).
  - The statuses are COMPLETE, MISSING and NOT_REQUIRED.
  - The market value is `null` if any asset is missing, never a partial sum.
  - AUM = cash + market value, `null` when either side is missing.
  - After maturity the PU is 0, with no observation id.
- `PortfolioAccounting.calculate_day/range` runs both chains for an ALL scope.
- `AccountingError` is internal; it is not a `DomainError`.

> Processing (contract/processing.ts):
> - `ProcessingScope` = "CASH" | "POSITIONS" | "ALL". `ProcessingRangeRequest` is {start_date, end_date, scope}, used by POST /processing/requests, POST /processing/requests/{id}/cancel-and-replace and GET /processing/preview?start_date&end_date&scope. `ProcessingRequest.scope` is required.
> - `ProcessingPreview` is {start_date, end_date, total_days, scope, predecessor_date, predecessor: {cash: PredecessorState, positions: PredecessorState}}. This replaces predecessor_state. PredecessorState = ZERO_STATE | VALID | STALE | NOT_PROCESSED.
> - `Issue.chain`: "CASH" | "POSITIONS" | null, set only on STALE_PREDECESSOR (your OQ-22 item 4).
> - FAILED jobs carry error_code CASH_PREDECESSOR_MISSING plus a pt-BR error_message (OQ-22 item 1). The mock refuses a CASH or ALL job when it starts, not when it is queued.
> - Proposal P19: GET /processing/outdated?start_date&end_date returns {start_date, end_date, cash_from, positions_from}. Per chain, it is the first day of the range that is stale or never processed. Days before the first ledger entry count as the zero state and are never outdated. Lançamentos uses it to prefill its new "Processar" button (user request today: queue processing from Lançamentos, prefilled with the stale part of the period).
>
> Portfolio (contract/portfolio.ts), GET /portfolio/summary?date returns:
> - {date,
>   cash: {status: Freshness, balance, published_at},
>   positions: {status: Freshness, published_at, market_value, valuation_status, position_pnl, trades_pnl, daily_pnl, cumulative_pnl, pnl_status},
>   aum: {status: Freshness, value}}
> - Freshness = "FRESH" | "STALE" | "NOT_PROCESSED". aum.value is null when either side is missing; aum.status is the less current of cash and positions.
> - I did not add the cash components (trades_cash_flow, deposits, withdrawals). They would fit as optional fields of `cash`; tell me if you want them.
> - GET /portfolio/positions and GET /portfolio/pnl keep publication + stale, now for the positions chain. Proposal: move them to Freshness too, so there is one convention.
>
> Ledger: P18, GET /ledger/entries?start_date&end_date&search&order=desc|asc, returns trades and cash movements in one list ordered by the backend. Each row has kind TRADE | CASH_MOVEMENT and a signed cash_amount. The user decided earlier today that this merge belongs in the backend. The write endpoints are unchanged.

The Lane I proposal (a user-approved switch from staging to day-by-day publishing) needs to be recorded by me as **OQ-23**. Before I record it, here is my review.

**I recommend adopting it, with four refinements:**

1. **The supersede check works per day, using the dirty markers.**
   - The revision counters can't show *which date* a write touched, so they can't tell "W after the current day" from "W on or before it".
   - Instead, in each day's transaction, the worker locks `calculation_state` and compares the job's markers with what it saw when it committed the previous day. The markers are cash, or positions plus valuation, according to the scope.
   - If a marker moved to a date ≤ D, a write landed on or before the current day, so the job stops: **SUPERSEDED**, with the remaining days NOT_RUN.
   - If the markers didn't move, any write was dated after D, and the job continues.
   - A job that started on a stale predecessor (marker before A) publishes every day flagged stale, as OQ-11 says, so it has nothing to fence.
2. **No new job status.** Use `SUPERSEDED` rather than "INTERRUPTED", keeping the 9 statuses of OQ-20.
3. **ALL scope:** both chains of day D commit in the same transaction, together with D's AUM recomputation.
4. **Fold it into migration 0002 inside PR #36,** which hasn't merged, rather than adding a 0003.
   - 0002 drops the `staged_*` tables, so `staged_cash_balances` is never created.
   - It adds `processing.request_days`.
   - OQ-21/OQ-22's text loses its "publicação tudo ou nada" wording in the same change.

**What changes in the docs:**
- spec §10.2 and §10.4;
- OQ-5, OQ-8, and OQ-20 items 3 and 4 ("Solicitar novo processamento" prefills from the first day not DONE);
- interfaces.md: `stage_*` and `publish_range` become one `write_day`, and steps 4–6 change;
- schema.sql/schema.md, the plan §6–7, and CLAUDE.md §2 ("Jobs").

**Impact on other lanes:**
- Lane E: the staging writer becomes `write_day`.
- Lane G: the processor commits day by day.
- Lane I: the per-day list in Processamentos.

**W0.3b is unaffected.** The invalidator and `ProcessingRequest` don't change. Its five commits still wait on your approval from the previous box.

- ✅ **Wrap-up**: PR #36 is open. W0.3b's five contract commits still wait for your approval. Lane I relayed your day-by-day publishing idea; I recommend it with a marker-based supersede check, no new status, and folding it into migration 0002.

PR #36 has merged. I opened the new branch `docs/oq23-day-by-day` from main (`0187611`) in worktree `.claude/worktrees/oq23-day-by-day`, and applied only the docs and schema parts of the saved draft. The old 0002 edits stay out. Nothing is committed and migration 0003 isn't written yet; I'm showing you the decision text first, as you asked.

**OQ-23 text for docs/open-questions.md:**

> | OQ-23 | Publish day by day instead of staging a range | *(your words)* Lane I session: "I think that maybe we dont need the staging anymore… (na fila, processando, erro x). What do you think?"; then "yes. Dont worry about the time…". Integrator session: "Yes: record OQ-23 (day-by-day publishing) with your four refinements, folded into migration 0002 on PR #36"; then "Separate PR: keep #36 as is, … record OQ-23 on a new branch with migration 0003; show me the decision text first." |

1. **No staging.** The worker processes [A, B] in calendar order, one transaction per day D. In that transaction it:
   - locks `calculation_state` and checks the claim token, the lease and the cancellation flag;
   - computes D for the job's scope;
   - writes D's published rows and recomputes D's AUM from the other chain;
   - advances the scope's markers that lie in [A, D] to D+1;
   - records D's status.
   
   The `staged_*` tables are dropped.
2. **One status per day**, in `processing.request_days(request_id, day, status, error_code, error_message, started_at, finished_at)`. The rows are created with the request. The statuses are PENDING **Na fila**, RUNNING **Processando**, DONE **Concluído**, DONE_WITH_ISSUES **Concluído com pendências**, FAILED **Erro**, and NOT_RUN **Não executado**.
3. **How a job stops:**
   - A technical error on D: D is FAILED, the later days are NOT_RUN, the job is FAILED, and A…D−1 stay published.
   - A cancellation: checked between days. The current day commits, the rest are NOT_RUN, and the job is CANCELLED.
   - A missing price: D is DONE_WITH_ISSUES and the job continues.
   - A missing cash predecessor (OQ-22): the job is still refused at start.
4. **Superseding uses the markers.** Before committing D, if any marker of the job's scope lies in [A, D], the job stops as SUPERSEDED and the remaining days are NOT_RUN.
   - A write dated after D doesn't stop it.
   - A marker before A (stale predecessor, OQ-11) doesn't stop it either.
   - There is no new job status; the nine stay.
5. **ALL scope:** both chains and the day's AUM commit in one transaction.
6. **Recovery** resumes from the first day not done, with the same marker check.
7. **Readers while a job runs:** each day is consistent with the one before it. A day not yet reprocessed keeps its previous result and its own freshness flag.
8. **"Solicitar novo processamento"** prefills from the first day not done to the original end.
9. **Schema change in migration 0003:** it drops `staged_*` and adds `request_days`. The per-input revision counters stay, but fencing now uses the markers.

**The same change also updates:**
- **Spec:** §10.2, §10.3 (what CANCELLED and SUPERSEDED mean), §10.4, §11.1 (the days listed in job details, "3 / 5 dias · erro em 16/10"), §13 item 10, §14, and the §9 note.
- **interfaces.md:** `PortfolioResultsWriter.write_positions_day` and `write_cash_day` replace `stage_*` and `publish_range`, and the processor loop, steps 4–6 and the marker rule become per day.
- **Amendment notes** on OQ-5, OQ-8 and OQ-20 items 3–4, plus the plan §6–7, CLAUDE.md §2 and module-engineer.md.
- **schema.sql and schema.md:** 18 tables.

**Once you approve the text:**
1. I write migration 0003, with upgrade and downgrade, and its tests.
2. I re-verify `schema.sql` on a fresh database, then run lint, the tests and the reviewer.
3. I show you both commits before committing.

**Separately:** the HTTP contract report is done (`lane-x-http-contract-mismatches.md` in the shared notes). It lists six points that need your decision. I'll bring them after OQ-23.

1. **Pagination is approved.** List endpoints take `?limit=&cursor=` and return `{items, next_cursor}`. Ledger entries are ordered by (date, time, seq), and jobs by (created_at, id).
2. **One ledger entry.**
   - A single `ledger.entries` table holds both trades and cash movements, with type TRADE or CASH, a check per type, and one shared `seq`.
   - Cash amounts are signed and cash entries now need a time. A trade's cash effect is computed, not stored.
   - Deletion takes no reason, and a multi-delete shares a batch id.
   - The routes are `GET`/`POST /ledger/entries`, with batch delete at `POST /ledger/entries/deletions`. They replace `/trades` and `/cash-movements`.
3. **Reference prices have no revisions.** There's one row per (asset, date, source). Correcting a price edits it in place, and removing one deletes it. `position_metrics` records the source of the reference PU it used instead of linking to the price row.

I've rewritten the decision text, now in [docs/oq23-25-draft.md](.claude/worktrees/oq23-day-by-day/docs/oq23-25-draft.md). It's a scratch file: nothing is recorded in open-questions.md yet, and the migration isn't touched.

**OQ-23: one status per date and chain.**
- No staging. A run publishes one date per transaction.
- `processing.chain_days(day, chain, status, request_id, error, updated_at)` is the only source of freshness. A date with no row is Não processado.
  - The statuses are Atualizado, Atualizado com pendências, Desatualizado, Processando and Erro.
  - There is no per-run day list.
- **A write dated W** marks the existing rows from W on as Desatualizado, in the same transaction:
  - a trade: Caixa and Posições;
  - a cash movement: Caixa;
  - a price: Posições.
  
  This replaces the dirty markers and all revision counters, which are dropped.
- **Processing a date D:**
  1. A short transaction marks D Processando.
  2. D's transaction locks D−1 and D, writes D and its AUM, and sets D's status: Atualizado if D−1 is current, otherwise Desatualizado with a Pendência.
  
  A write that arrives mid-run waits on those locks and then marks dates stale; the run never stops for it.
- **Stopping:** an error at D leaves D in Erro and the run Falhou. A cancel commits the current date and the run ends Cancelado. Later dates are untouched in both cases.
- **Runs** have eight statuses; Obsoleto is gone. Recovery resumes after the last date the run committed. The retry prefills from the date that failed, or from the first date not reached.
- **Migration 0003** backfills a status for every date already published, from the old markers.

**OQ-24: one ledger entry** (your relayed decision, written out).
- One `ledger.entries` table with a check per type and a shared `seq`.
- A signed `cash_amount` for cash only. A trade's cash effect is computed, not stored.
- No delete reason; a multi-delete shares a `deletion_batch_id`.
- The `/ledger/entries` routes replace `/trades` and `/cash-movements`.

**OQ-25: prices without revisions.**
- One row per (asset, date, source). A correction updates it in place and a removal deletes it.
- `position_metrics` stores `reference_source` instead of linking to the price row.
- "Ver revisões" is dropped from the UI.

**Two choices of mine to check:**
1. **A date whose predecessor has an issue still counts as current.** If D−1 is Atualizado com pendências (a missing price, say), D can still become Atualizado, because the issue is about data, not about recomputing.
2. **What the reads show.** Every read and both badges take the date's status directly, so a badge can say Caixa: Processando. The frontend's three-value Freshness (FRESH/STALE/NOT_PROCESSED) would then become these status values. I'll settle that in the HTTP contract with lane I.

- ✅ **Wrap-up**: The OQ-23, OQ-24 and OQ-25 decision text is drafted in docs/oq23-25-draft.md on branch docs/oq23-day-by-day. Nothing is recorded in open-questions.md yet and the migration is untouched.

### 👤 User · 2026-10-18 23:49

why are restoring superseded? what is downgrade?


#### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-18 23:49


These are two separate mechanisms, so here's each in plain terms.

**What a "downgrade" is.** Each migration (0001, 0002, 0003) has two scripts:
- **upgrade** moves the database forward to the new structure. That's the one that matters: it builds your new tables and moves existing data into them.
- **downgrade** undoes it, taking the database back to the previous version (0003 back to 0002). It's an emergency rollback for developers, used when a release has to be reverted. Users never see it, and it isn't part of the product.

**Why SUPERSEDED came up.** You removed the SUPERSEDED status. In the upgrade, any old run marked SUPERSEDED becomes FAILED, because the new schema no longer allows that value. The reviewer pointed out that the downgrade doesn't turn those runs back into SUPERSEDED, so a rollback wouldn't be a faithful reversal. It only affects the rollback script; SUPERSEDED isn't coming back into the product.

**My recommendation.** That rollback script is the most complex part of 0003. It has to rebuild the two ledger tables, the price revisions, the markers and the staging tables. And there's no real data yet: this is a challenge project before delivery. The project rules ask for a downgrade "only where safe and documented", so the simpler, safer choice is:
- Downgrade 0003 only on a database with no business data. It refuses otherwise, the same way the 0001 downgrade already does.
- It rebuilds the 0002 structure empty and carries no data back.

That deletes the data-carry-back code: the SUPERSEDED restore, the marker reconstruction, and the ledger and price round-trips. The test that the rollback returns exactly 0002's structure stays.

- ✅ **Wrap-up**: A downgrade is the developer rollback script that takes the database from 0003 back to 0002; the SUPERSEDED restore lived only there. My recommendation is to make 0003's rollback work only on an empty database, which removes all of that carry-back code.

**What the migration and schema now do:**
- **Your rollback decision:** the 0003 downgrade only runs on a database without business data. It rebuilds the 0002 structure empty and carries nothing back; if forced, it deletes the business rows instead. Recorded as OQ-26 item 6. All the old carry-back code is gone.
- **OQ-26 recorded:** the five follow-ups (pagination, no cash description, zero state per chain, restoring a date left Processando, Pendências replaced per date) and the rollback policy, with your words.
- **Schema:** `processing.date_status` gains `portfolio_previous` and `cash_previous`. They hold a chain's status while it's Processando so it can be put back, with CHECKs that allow them only while RUNNING.
- **Upgrade carry-over (my choices, written up in `schema.md`):**
  - The Pendência "contrato vencido com posição aberta" now also counts toward "Atualizado com pendências".
  - Old runs that were cancelled or failed published nothing, so they lose their progress date.
  - A SUPERSEDED run becomes FAILED, with a clearer pt-BR message.
- **Concurrency in the docs:** writes and runs lock dates in the same ascending order, so they can't deadlock. Every worker write checks the claim token, so a crashed worker that comes back can't overwrite anything.
- **One reading of mine:** saving a price where one already exists for that asset, date and source replaces it as a correction. I took that from your words "delete a price when we recieve a new one".

## Major

**N1. The rules for `*_previous` can produce rows that the CHECKs reject, or a wrong status.**
- **Where:** `docs/schema.sql:172-178`, `0003_one_status_per_date.sql:11-18`, `docs/interfaces.md:63`, `:73`, `:78`, and spec line 296.
- **Rule:** OQ-26 item 4, and OQ-23 item 3 ("Dates without a status stay Não processado").
- **Scenario 1, the first run on a date:**
  1. D has no row. Step 1 inserts it with `portfolio='RUNNING'`, `portfolio_previous=NULL` and `cash=NULL`.
  2. The worker dies, and the run is finalized after the lost lease. The restore sets `portfolio` back to NULL.
  3. Both chains are now NULL, which violates `date_status_not_empty`. The finalization fails, and D stays `RUNNING`, the same symptom as M6.
- **Scenario 2, a write on an unprocessed date:** a write lands while D is `RUNNING` with `previous=NULL`. `interfaces.md:63` says the kept status "becomes `STALE`". After a restore, a date that was never processed reads "Desatualizado" instead of "Não processado".
- **Scenario 3, recovery:** recovery resumes at D, which is still `RUNNING`. Step 1 copies the current status into `*_previous`. That puts `'RUNNING'` there, which `date_status_*_previous_check` rejects. Recovery fails.
- **Fix:** specify these three points in interfaces.md, the spec and the plan:
  - if restoring would leave both chains NULL, delete the row instead;
  - the invalidator turns `*_previous` into `STALE` only when it is not NULL;
  - step 1 le

> Lane I → X: the frontend contract is now in PR #41 (https://github.com/josepaulolaport/maravi_di1/pull/41, branch frontend/contract-review, head 7cdb119, base main). The user asked that we both hold the same picture. Below is what the frontend implements. Please reply with every point where your record differs, and with anything you have that's missing here.
>
> Files: frontend/src/api/contract/{common,asset,prices,ledger,portfolio,processing}.ts, endpoints in frontend/src/api/endpoints.ts, and the proposals table in frontend/src/api/README.md (P1–P21).
>
> 1. Lists and pagination (P21)
> - Paginated lists are Page<T> = {items, next_cursor}. next_cursor is opaque and null on the last page; there is no total.
> - Query parameters: ?limit=&cursor=, with limit 1–200 and a default of 50.
> - Paginated endpoints: GET /ledger/entries (keyset order (date, time, seq)) and GET /processing/requests (newest first, by (created_at, id)).
> - The UI reads 50 entries or 20 jobs per page, with "Carregar mais".
> - Every other list is still {items}.
>
> 2. Ledger (P18, P20, P7)
> - One entry: {id, type: TRADE|CASH, date, time (required on both types), seq (shared), cash_amount (signed, computed by the backend), created_at}.
> - TRADE adds: asset_id, asset_code, instrument_type_code, signed_quantity, execution_pu, execution_rate, input_kind.
> - CASH adds nothing. cash_amount > 0 is an aporte, < 0 a retirada. There is no description.
> - GET /ledger/entries?start_date&end_date&search&order(asc|desc)&limit&cursor, ordered by (date, time, seq).
> - POST /ledger/entries:
>   - TRADE: {type:'TRADE', asset_id, date, time, signed_quantity, input_kind, execution_pu | execution_rate}
>   - CASH: {type:'CASH', date, time, cash_amount}, signed, at most 2 places, never zero.
>   - Returns the entry with 201.
> - POST /ledger/entries/deletions {ids}: all or nothing, no reason, 204; an unknown id returns 404 and deletes nothing.
> - Gone: /trades and /cash-movements (list, create and delete).
> - The user also wants one database table (ledger.entries). That one is yours.
>
> 3. Reference prices (P8)
> - No revisions: one price per (asset, date, source).
> - ReferencePrice {id, asset_id, reference_date, source: MANUAL|B3, input_kind, pu, rate, created_at}.
> - GET /reference-prices?asset_id&start_date&end_date returns ReferencePriceDay {reference_date, preferred, sources[]}. MANUAL wins over B3. Exact date only; a day with no price is absent.
> - POST /reference-prices overwrites the MANUAL price in place (same id, 200) or creates it (201).
> - DELETE /reference-prices/{id} deletes the price (204).
> - Gone: revision, superseded_at, /reference-prices/revisions and "Ver revisões".
> - PositionRow.reference_price_id still points to the price row. Traceability to the value used at publication is lost by design.
>
> 4. Portfolio (OQ-21 draft, two chains)
> - Freshness = FRESH | STALE | NOT_PROCESSED, per chain only, never per metric.
> - GET /portfolio/summary?date returns {date, cash, positions, aum}:
>   - cash: {status, balance, trades_cash_flow, deposits (≥ 0), withdrawals (≥ 0, subtracted), published_at}
>   - positions: {status, published_at, market_value, valuation_status, position_pnl, trades_pnl, daily_pnl, cumulative_pnl, pnl_status}
>   - aum: {status, value}. value is null if either side is missing; status is stale or not processed if either side is.
> - GET /portfolio/positions?date returns {date, status, published_at, items}.
> - GET /portfolio/pnl?start_date&end_date returns items {date, daily_pnl, cumulative_pnl, pnl_status, status}, published days only.
> - What a write makes stale: a trade, both chains; a cash entry, cash only; a reference price, positions only.
> - UI: only two data-status badges (Caixa, Posições). There is no badge per KPI and no stale or incomplete notice cards.
>
> 5. Processing
> - scope = CASH | POSITIONS | ALL, required on POST /processing/requests, on the preview (GET /processing/preview?start_date&end_date&scope) and on cancel-and-replace. It is echoed on every ProcessingRequest.
> - ProcessingPreview.predecessor = {cash, positions}, each ZERO_STATE | VALID | STALE | NOT_PROCESSED.
> - Issue.chain is set for STALE_PREDECESSOR and null otherwise.
> - OQ-22: a CASH or ALL job whose day before has no published cash ends FAILED with error_code CASH_PREDECESSOR_MISSING. Positions still run after a gap (OQ-11).
> - P19 (GET /processing/outdated) is dropped.
> - JobStatus is still the 9 old values. OQ-23 (one status per chain per date, no SUPERSEDED, 8 run statuses) is NOT implemented; I'm waiting for your frozen shape.
>
> 6. Docs I can't edit; these are for you
> - Spec §5 and root CLAUDE.md §2: drop revisions ("traceable to its revision").
> - frontend/CLAUDE.md:
>   - Ativos menu becomes "Corrigir, Remover".
>   - Lançamentos uses one modal, not drawers.
>   - Carteira shows two data-status badges, not "stale and incomplete markers with links".
> - GAP-I-1 is reversed: cash entries have a time.
> - interfaces.md and schema.sql: the unified ledger, pagination, and no price revisions.

X3 is ready on branch `x3-app-factory`, in worktree `.claude/worktrees/x3-app`, from main `0bd044b`. Commit 1 is staged and nothing is committed yet.

| # | Commit | Files |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `feat(composition): add the app factory with a database health check` (staged, 7 files, +213) | `main.py`, `composition/health.py`, `composition/routers.py`, `composition/dependencies.py`, a 503 title in `composition/errors.py`, `tests/unit/composition/test_app.py`, `tests/integration/test_app_health.py` |
| 2 | `build(infra): trace greenlets and threads in coverage` | `backend/pyproject.toml`: one setting, `concurrency = ["greenlet", "thread"]` |

**What it does:**
- **`create_app(settings=None)`** builds the app as a uvicorn factory: `uvicorn di1.main:create_app --factory`. Nothing reads the environment when the module is imported, as the config rule requires. On startup it creates platform's engine and session factory, and at shutdown it disposes of the engine. The problem+json error handlers are registered.
- **`GET /health`** answers `200 {"status": "ok"}` when PostgreSQL answers `SELECT 1`. Otherwise it answers `503` problem+json with the title "Serviço indisponível" and the detail "O banco de dados não está acessível.". The response leaks no part of the error, and the error is logged.
- **`composition/routers.py`** is the empty place where module routers will be included.
- **`composition/dependencies.py`** gives each request its own Unit of Work.
- **Commit 2:** coverage reported `/health`'s error branch as never run, even though a test asserts it. The cause is SQLAlchemy's async engine running in greenlets, and this setting makes the report accurate.

**Tests:**
- Unit: the 503 answer, with no password, host or exception name in the body; problem+json for an unknown route; the engine disposed at shutdown.
- Integration on PostgreSQL 16: `/health` returns 200, and a request's Unit of Work reaches the database.

**Checks:**
- `make lint-backend`: "All checks passed!", "132 files already formatted", "Success: no issues found in 131 source files".
- `make test-backend`: "452 passed, 23 skipped", and `health.py` is at 100% coverage. The 23 skipped are lane H's broker tests.

#### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-19 00:49


Lane I is asking about something the docs don't define. OQ-26 says AUM's status is "the less current" of cash and positions, but never ranks the six statuses. That's a decision for you, not me.

Their proposed order, from least to most current:

**NOT_PROCESSED < FAILED < STALE < RUNNING < FRESH_WITH_ISSUES < FRESH**

The order reads well to me, with one question: **RUNNING**. While a date is Processando, its published data is the previous version. Putting RUNNING above STALE means a date that was stale before being reprocessed reads better than Desatualizado for that moment. If you want AUM never to look more current than its data really is, RUNNING would sit just below STALE instead.

What commit 5 exposes:
- **The pure chains:** `PositionsEngine`, `CashCalculator`, `ValuationCalculator`, `PortfolioAccounting`, and their values.
- **`PortfolioResultsWriter`:** `write_positions_day(day, valuation, asset_pnl, pnl, *, uow)` and `write_cash_day(day, *, uow)`, one date per call, with no `publish_range`.
- **`PortfolioReader`:** `get_previous_positions(before_date) -> PreviousPositionsDay(state, reference_pus, cumulative_pnl)` and `get_previous_cash(before_date)`.
- **`PortfolioQueryService`:** `get_summary`, `get_position_table` and `get_pnl_series`. Their read models mirror the frozen HTTP contract at `57b4df0`:
  - AUM is a plain value with no status (OQ-28);
  - a position row traces its price by source, not by id (OQ-25);
  - a chain status of `None` means not processed.

**Choices of mine to check:**
- `PreviousPositionsDay` bundles the D−1 reference PUs and the cumulative P&L. Processing needs both to compute D's P&L (OQ-9), and interfaces.md names only the readers.
- The read models use `ChainStatus | None` instead of a separate six-value enum. The HTTP layer maps `None` to `NOT_PROCESSED`.

Understood: the Carteira shows the PU and rate from the current price table. That's the last of the three, so here is the text I'd record as **OQ-30**, before I touch the docs, contracts or schema:

> | OQ-30 | Reviewer gaps on W0.3c: reference PU and rate on a processed date; a positions run with no predecessor; cancel-and-replace | "why does position_metrics store PU? Shouldnt PU be on price?…"; then "The current price table". Predecessor: "Refuse at start (Recommended)". Cancel-and-replace: "we shouldnt have the possibility to cancel and replace. Remove that. let the user only cancel or queue as separate actions" |

1. **Prices live only in the price table.**
   - The Carteira shows each date's reference PU, rate and source from the current preferred price (MANUAL, else B3, exact date). After maturity the PU is 0.
   - `position_metrics` stops storing `reference_pu` and `reference_source`. It keeps the computed `financial_total` and P&L.
   - After a price correction, the date shows the new PU next to the old figures and reads **Desatualizado** until it's reprocessed.
   - OQ-9 changes: D's position P&L takes the D−1 PU from the price table. If that price changed after D−1 was processed, D−1 is Desatualizado, and D is too, by the predecessor rule.
   - Schema: migration **0004** drops the two columns.
2. **Missing positions predecessor:** a POSITIONS or ALL run whose A−1 has no published positions, while a trade is dated on or before A−1, is refused at start. It ends `FAILED` with `POSITIONS_PREDECESSOR_MISSING` and publishes nothing, as cash does (OQ-22).
3. **No cancel-and-replace:**
   - `POST /processing/requests/{id}/cancel-and-replace` and `ProcessingService.cancel_and_replace` are removed. The user cancels, or queues a new request, as separate actions.
   - This reverses OQ-20 item 5. "Solicitar novo processamento" stays.

**Where it lands:**
- **This PR (contracts):** the predecessor reader no longer returns D−1 PUs, and the writer no longer stores the PU or source.
- **Docs:** OQ-30 and its propagation.
- **Next PR:** migration 0004.

#### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-19 02:18


Lead Backend relayed a big decision of yours: **remove staleness from the project entirely**. Before I record anything, here is the OQ-31 text. There's also one conflict with your OQ-30 answer that you need to settle.

**OQ-31: no staleness; "Processado" with the processing time**

1. **Nothing is marked stale.**
   - Saving or deleting a ledger entry or a price only saves the fact. Ledger and Prices no longer call Processing.
   - `FinancialResultInvalidator`, the "mark stale in the same transaction" rule, the STALE status and the "desatualizado" wording are all removed.
   - A processed date keeps its figures until you process it again. It's noted next to deviation D3 that the user reprocesses after changing history.
2. **Date statuses per chain:**
   - `PROCESSED` "Processado";
   - `PROCESSED_WITH_ISSUES` "Processado com pendências";
   - `RUNNING` "Processando";
   - `FAILED` "Erro";
   - none: "Não processado".
   
   The codes are renamed from FRESH and FRESH_WITH_ISSUES as well. I agree with Lead Backend here: "FRESH" implies a freshness the system no longer tracks.
3. **Processing times.** `processing.date_status` gains `cash_processed_at` and `portfolio_processed_at`. Each is set when a run commits that chain for the date, and stays NULL if never processed. Every read exposes them per date and chain, and the UI shows "Processado em dd/mm/aaaa hh:mm".
4. **The predecessor rule without staleness.** D is published normally when D−1 is processed (with or without issues) or is the zero state.
5. **Schema.** This folds into migration 0004 in PR #50, which hasn't merged: the STALE value goes from the CHECKs, the two timestamp columns are added, and existing STALE values become PROCESSED. PR #49 is also cleaned of the stale wording and the invalidator. That changes a contract lanes C and D already use; Lead Backend is telling them.

**The conflict.** Lead Backend's text says a positions run whose D−1 is not processed should still run, with P&L null and a Pendência "dia anterior não processado". But in OQ-30, a few minutes earlier, you chose to **refuse** a positions run at start when A−1 was never published and trades exist before it (`POSITIONS_PREDECESSOR_MISSING`). Within a run, every D−1 is the run's own previous date, so the question only arises at the start, A−1.

**OQ-33 — Asset HTTP rules (lane A, PR #47)**

Your words (lane A session, 2026-10-19, multiple-choice picks):
- On code normalisation: "Trim + upper-case (Recommended)".
- On an unknown `product_id`: "422 on product_id (Recommended)".
- On `search`: "Case+accent-insensitive (Recommended)".
- On `last_reference`: "Always null for now". This was not the recommended option, which was "Port + CCR to lane X".
- In this session, asked whether the 422 rule covers any unknown id in a request body: "ok".

Decision:
1. **Codes:** an instrument or product `code` is trimmed and upper-cased before validation and storage. For example, `' di1f27 '` is stored as `DI1F27`. Uniqueness stays case-insensitive.
2. **Unknown ids:**
   - An id in the request body that doesn't exist (`product_id` on `POST /instruments`, `asset_class_id` on `POST /products`, and so on) answers 422 `VALIDATION_ERROR` on that field. It can be combined with other field errors.
   - 404 `NOT_FOUND` is only for the resource in the URL path.
3. **Search:** `search` on `GET /products` and `GET /instruments` is a substring match, ignoring case and accents. It matches products on code, name and asset class name, and instruments on code. Accent folding is done in Python because the catalogue is small; there is no `unaccent` extension or migration.
4. **`last_reference`:** `Instrument.last_reference` is always `null` for now, and the UI shows "—". It will be filled once Prices exposes a latest-observation read, through a contract change to lane X.

Updates in the same change:
- `interfaces.md`, Asset section and error rules.
- Spec §11 (Ativos) and §14.
- `CLAUDE.md`, if needed.

It would go on its own docs branch, not on PR #49, which is already large.

**M1. W2 `backend/migrations/sql/0004_no_staleness.sql:26-34, 38-45, 50, 55`: a `STALE` chain with no published row becomes `PROCESSED`, with a NULL processing time.**
- **Rule:** OQ-31 item 3 says `*_processed_at` is "NULL when never processed". Item 4 says a run that did not commit D−1 leaves D−1 not processed.
- **Scenario:** in 0003, a run fails on 10-04, so the date is `FAILED` with no rows. A trade dated 10-01 is then saved, and the 0003 invalidation marks 10-04 `STALE`, because `FAILED` "has a status".

  - A later run starting on 10-05 passes the status half of the predecessor check. It is saved only by the `get_previous_*` docstring contract.

- **Fix:** map `STALE` (and `*_previous = 'STALE'`) to `PROCESSED` / `PROCESSED_WITH_ISSUES` only when that chain has a published row on the date (`portfolio_metrics` for portfolio, `cash_balances` for cash). **OQ-32, ledger hard delete (draft):**

User's words (lane C session, 2026-10-19): "why softdelete the ledger? Didnt we agree on just deleting it?", then "ok, lets do the hard delete".

1. **Hard delete.**
   - Deleting entries is a real `DELETE` from `ledger.entries`.
   - `delete_entries(ids)` runs in one transaction, all or nothing.
   - An unknown or already-deleted id answers 404 `NOT_FOUND`, and nothing is deleted. This settles GAP-C-1.
   - There is still no edit and no delete reason. This reverses OQ-24 item 4 (soft delete and `deletion_batch_id`) and spec §6 "Exclusão lógica".
2. **No audit trail of deleted entries.**
   - A deleted entry no longer exists anywhere.
   - Results already processed keep their figures until you process the dates again (OQ-31).
   - Soft delete was our own addition, not a requirement of the challenge, so this is not a challenge deviation.
3. **"Active" goes away.** Every "active entry/trade" rule, such as the OQ-31 zero state and the OQ-10 expected assets, now just means "entry/trade".
4. **Schema:** folded into migration 0004 (PR #50, still unmerged).
   - On `ledger.entries`, drop `deleted_at`, `deletion_batch_id` and the CHECK `entries_deletion_batch_tied`.
   - Rebuild `entries_ordered_active_idx` as `entries_ordered_idx (entry_date, entry_time, seq)`.
   - Rebuild `entries_asset_date_active_idx` as `entries_trade_asset_date_idx (asset_id, entry_date, seq) WHERE type = 'TRADE'`.
   - Rows already soft-deleted are physically deleted by the upgrade.
   - The downgrade brings the columns back empty.
5. **Docs updated with it:**
   - OQ-24 and OQ-26 notes, spec §6 and §14;
   - interfaces "Ledger";
   - schema.md decision 3;
   - CLAUDE.md §2 "History is immutable", reworded to: never edited, deleted only by an explicit user action;
   - plan §5.C.

**OQ-34: Pricing calculator selection and error fields (lane B, PR #52)**

Your words (lane B session, 2026-10-19, multiple-choice picks):
- On which instruments the DI1 calculator prices: "Product code DI1 (Recommended)". The other option was "Catalogue hierarchy".
- On who maps error field names: "Callers remap (Recommended)". The other option was "Keep all as is".

Decision:
1. **Calculator selection:** `PricingService` prices an instrument with the DI1 calculator only when its product code is `DI1`, compared case-insensitively. Any other product, or an instrument without a maturity or face value, is a 422 `VALIDATION_ERROR` on `asset_id`. A product registered under another code cannot be priced, so users must register DI1 as `DI1`.
2. **Error fields:**
   - Pricing reports errors on its own field names: `asset_id` (renamed from `instrument_id`), `reference_date`, `pu` and `rate`.
   - Each caller renames them to its own request fields. Ledger maps `reference_date` → `date` (the frozen frontend contract's name), `pu` → `execution_pu` and `rate` → `execution_rate`. Prices already uses the same names.
3. **Errors module:** the calculator raises `DomainError` directly, and `pricing/domain/errors.py` is gone.

This updates `interfaces.md` "Pricing", the `pricing/public.py` docstring and spec §14. It would go on PR #54's branch, or on a new branch if #54 has already merged.

Lane G says you decided a new rule in its session that changes OQ-31 item 7 (ALL runs).

When one chain fails on a date D, either because the day before wasn't processed or from a technical error:
- **Failing chain:** shows "Erro" on D, and its later dates aren't touched.
- **Other chain:** keeps going to the end of the range. Dates are still processed one at a time, both chains in the same transaction.
- **AUM:** stays empty on any date where either chain has no published value, as today.
- **Run status:** "Concluído com pendências" (`COMPLETED_WITH_ISSUES`), with an error code and message set. It ends `FAILED` only if both chains fail.

What lane G sent is a cut-off paraphrase, not your exact words. I've asked for the exact text before recording it as OQ-36.

A date's `aum` is null when the other chain on that date is FAILED or not processed, for example after a Tudo run where one chain failed. Your mock should return `aum: null` for those dates. The UI already shows null AUM as unavailable, so only the mock should need a change.

> How I read it for interfaces.md "Predecessor rule, per day" (lane G implements):
> - D−1 PROCESSED or PROCESSED_WITH_ISSUES → compute D.
> - D−1 FAILED or RUNNING → fail D, without asking Ledger.
> - Only when the chain has nothing processed before D (D−1 has no status, and no earlier date has one for that chain) → call has_entries_on_or_before: entry_type TRADE for positions, None for cash. False → zero state, compute D. True → fail D.
> - If D−1 has no status but an earlier date does (a gap), fail D without asking Ledger: every day must be processed.
> Please confirm this reading with the user and record it. Lane C side: 418e5d4 on PR #48 implements has_entries_on_or_before (one EXISTS query) and documents it as this first-day fallback only.

Lead Backend relayed your answers on the last two open #54 points. Neither is in the docs yet.

- **N3, zero state:** a chain starts from zero on a date D only if no earlier date has any status for that chain, and there is no entry of its type up to the day before D. The existing checks still come first: a failed or running day before, or a gap after an earlier processed date, fails D without asking Ledger.
- **m3, cancelled ALL run:** a cancelled ALL run keeps the failed date and failed chain. Its retry offers that chain's scope from the failed date to the original end. A plain ALL retry is offered too.

The processing contract docstring already reflects m3; architecture tests pass (123).

The OQ-43 follow-ups are in [PR #84](https://github.com/josepaulolaport/maravi_di1/pull/84), one docs commit (`e71a890`) that passed the pre-commit hook. It records three edge cases of the recovery cap. They are my reading of what the worker already does, not new decisions:

- **Cancelling run:** a run you were cancelling that then hits the recovery cap ends "Falhou" (`RECOVERY_EXHAUSTED`), not "Cancelado".
- **Retry after the cap:** such a run has no failed date or chain, so "Solicitar novo processamento" starts the day after the last processed date (or at the start, if none) and runs to the original end.
- **No worker running:** a re-dispatch that no worker picks up doesn't count toward the cap. With no worker running, the run is re-sent every lease period until one claims it. This is now documented.

The OQ-14 note now says that only writes are rejected after today: price writes, trades and cash movements. Lead Backend knows the PR is up for the reviewer.


A session named "btw: how much until we are done?" relayed your words "we dont need multiple workers just one" for me to record. What it says should follow from them conflicts with things already decided and built:

- **Leases go away.** OQ-43, which you decided earlier today, caps recovery attempts that are triggered by an expired lease. #78 (merged) implements leases, claim tokens and that cap.
- **Recovery happens only at worker startup.** Today the dispatcher recovers a dead worker's run on its regular tick, without waiting for a restart. Startup-only recovery would make OQ-43's cap and `RECOVERY_EXHAUSTED` meaningless.
- **The lease and claim-token columns become unused.** They are live in the merged worker.

Nothing is written yet. Here is the draft I'd record as OQ-45:

> **OQ-45, one processing worker.** Your words: "we dont need multiple workers just one".
> 1. The system runs exactly one processing worker (one `worker` service in Compose, plus the outbox relay). No test or design covers concurrent workers.
> 2. Dispatch is unchanged: the request and its outbox message are saved in one transaction, the relay publishes, and the worker consumes from RabbitMQ. Only one run is active at a time, and the database constraints stay.

The rest depends on how you answer the questions below.

OQ-45 is recorded in [PR #85](https://github.com/josepaulolaport/maravi_di1/pull/85); this is the text, for your review:

1. Exactly one processing worker runs.
2. Dispatch is unchanged: the request and its outbox message are saved together, the relay publishes, and the worker consumes from RabbitMQ. Duplicate-message protection stays, because one worker can still receive a message twice.
3. There is no lease, no claim-token fencing and no recovery cap. This reverses OQ-43, and `RECOVERY_EXHAUSTED` is retired.
4. Recovery happens when the worker starts. Dates left "Processando" are restored first. Then a run left dispatched or running resumes after its last processed date, and a run left cancelling ends "Cancelado". This order is my reading.
5. The dispatcher stays its own process, with one copy. You didn't say to fold it into the worker, so that's my default.
6. The lease and claim-token columns stay in the database, unused, with no new migration. That's my choice, to avoid a schema change hours before delivery.
7. `PROCESSING_ERROR` stays.

The PR has two docs-only commits, both passed the pre-commit hook, and I removed every rule that assumed several workers.

The merged worker code (#78) still has leases and the cap until the Wave 4 worker session's follow-up PR lands; I gave that session the details. x-wiring will keep one worker in Compose, and lane I will drop `RECOVERY_EXHAUSTED` from the frontend.

OQ-46 is recorded in [PR #91](https://github.com/josepaulolaport/maravi_di1/pull/91). It's docs only, two commits, and the pre-commit hook passed on both.

The design you accepted:
- **No dispatcher process.** Saving a request writes it as queued, plus a message that only means "there is work".
- **The worker drains the queue.** On each message, and at startup, the single worker takes the oldest queued request from PostgreSQL, runs it, and repeats until none are left. A lost or duplicate message does no harm.
- **The "dispatched" status is retired.** Requests go straight from queued to running. It stays in the database, unused, with no migration.

These follow from the change, and the record marks them as my readings:
- The "cancelling" status and the active-run slot column also stay unused in the database.
- Recovery at worker startup runs before the queue is drained, and your cancel-at-restart rule is unchanged.
- The "Enviado" and "Cancelando" labels never show, so the frontend needs no change.
- If a cancel and the worker's pickup hit the same queued request, whichever commits first wins.

#91 must merge together with the code that removes the dispatcher, so main never describes a system it doesn't have yet. Lead Backend and the merger know.

One loose end: #86's README says `docker compose up` starts a dispatcher. I'll remove that once #86 and the code land, and I asked Lead Backend whether to do it in #86 or a separate PR.

PR #91 (OQ-46) now records why RabbitMQ stays, quoting your "Wouldnt the doorbell make rabbitmq superfluous?" and "lets go ahead with it". New commit `af4ab95` adds it as OQ-46 item 10, with a line each in `docs/interfaces.md` and the spec.

The recorded reasoning:
- **Where the truth lives:** PostgreSQL holds the requests, their order and their status. RabbitMQ only wakes the worker.
- **Delivery:** each request is saved before its message is published, through the outbox with publisher confirms.
- **Lost messages:** the next message, or the next time the worker starts, picks up anything still queued.
- **No polling:** the worker is pushed awake.
- **Why it's worth keeping:** challenge §8 asks for messaging where it adds value. Here the API answers at once and processing runs in a separate process.
- **Rejected alternative:** PostgreSQL LISTEN/NOTIFY or polling.

Two smaller changes in the same commit:
- **Message payload:** a request ID in the message is for logs only; the worker never uses it to choose a run.
- **Active-run slot:** I recorded the `active_request_id` column as unused, with no migration. Otherwise the worker would have to start setting it, which is the bigger change.

The README edit waits for #86 to merge: removing "dispatcher" from the service list and adding a short "why RabbitMQ" paragraph. After that, main needs to be merged into #91, and you'll have to run that merge because the hook blocks it for me. Lead Backend knows #91 is the OQ-46 PR.

**📨 Message from session “Wave 4 processing worker”**

> wave4-worker → lane X: OQ-46 Processing is PR #93. A few names and behaviours for #91 to match:
> - The take is `PortfolioRangeProcessor.take_next()`, not `ProcessingQueue.take_next()`. The loop is `PortfolioRangeProcessor.drain()`, and startup is `recover_at_startup()` (recovery, then the drain).
> - The doorbell message type and queue stay `processing.run_request`, payload `{}`. It's written by `OutboxDoorbell` in `enqueue_range`'s transaction.
> - `take_next` selects again until it locks a QUEUED row or none exists. A cancel and a take on the same row: whichever commits first wins. Nothing is taken while a run is RUNNING.
> - A cancel of a RUNNING run sets only `cancel_requested_at`, and the status stays RUNNING. A second cancel answers 409 "O cancelamento deste processamento já foi solicitado.".
> - No `calculation_state` use. DISPATCHED, CANCELLING and `dispatched_at` are unused; `public.py` docstrings say so. Recovery still handles rows left in those statuses from before.
